**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 2 ("Then, the next moment, I saw Miss Mia run to the left!") and Testimony 4 ("She turned, and ran for the door!"). Redd White first states Mia ran to the left, then states she ran for the door. These directions are contradictory. It's possible to run left and then turn towards a door, but the immediate sequence presented creates a spatial inconsistency.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 7: "If Mia Fey runs to the right then Mia Fey is moving to the right" - This is the opposite of what's being described, so it doesn't apply.

While Prop 8 is relevant to Mia's actions, it doesn't directly resolve the spatial contradiction. The core issue is the conflicting directions given by Redd White.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through basic spatial reasoning. Running "left" and running "for the door" are distinct directions. If someone runs left, it doesn't automatically mean they are running towards a door. The sequence of events described by White is spatially inconsistent.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

Testimony 3 states: "But... from under the cherry tree... I feel like... I saw something red." Evidence 7 (Bigg Building Pamphlet) states the cherry tree is *inside* the Bigg Building viewing platform. Testimony 4 states: "Umm... That's right, someone was walking towards me from the other side of that stand." Evidence 7 also states the concession stand is *in front of* the Bigg Building viewing platform. This creates a spatial contradiction: If Kay saw something red from *under* the cherry tree (inside the viewing platform), how could she simultaneously see someone walking from the *other side* of the concession stand (outside the viewing platform)? The two locations are spatially incompatible given the testimonies.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Several spatial propositions are relevant:

*   Prop 1: The concession stand is in front of the Bigg Building viewing platform.
*   Prop 2: The cherry tree is inside the Bigg Building viewing platform.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which and explain**

Props 1 and 2 directly highlight the contradiction. Prop 1 establishes the concession stand as being *outside* the viewing platform, while Prop 2 places the cherry tree *inside* it. Testimony 3 places Kay's observation point *under* the cherry tree (inside), while Testimony 4 places the person she saw *beyond* the concession stand (outside). **The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

Testimony 3 states: "The only exit from the storeroom is the hatch that leads to the viewing platform." Testimony 4 then states: "Ms. Crane went out to the viewing platform, where she was attacked by Kay Faraday." Evidence 17 shows a photograph of a figure in a red raincoat in the storeroom, walking towards a ladder. This implies an exit *other* than the hatch to the viewing platform. This creates a spatial contradiction: if the hatch is the *only* exit, how could the figure in the raincoat be in the storeroom and heading towards a ladder?

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 3: The hatch is inside the storeroom.
Prop 4: The viewing platform is inside the Bigg Building.
Prop 10: The viewing platform is in front of the hatch.
Prop 11: The storeroom is in front of the viewing platform.

**Step 3: Propositions revealing the contradiction**

Props 3, 4, 10, and 11 all establish a spatial relationship between the storeroom, hatch, and viewing platform. Testimony 3 asserts the hatch is the *only* exit. Evidence 17, however, depicts someone *inside* the storeroom, moving towards a ladder, implying an alternative exit. This contradicts the exclusivity of the hatch as the only exit, as suggested by Testimony 3. The ladder implies a route *upwards* from the storeroom, not through the hatch to the viewing platform.

**Step 4: Spatial Reasoning**

The contradiction lies in the implied spatial pathways. Testimony 3 limits the exit to the hatch, leading to the viewing platform. Evidence 17 shows a different spatial pathway – a ladder within the storeroom, suggesting an alternative route out. The ladder's presence directly challenges the assertion that the hatch is the sole exit.

Prop 15: If the camera blocks the view of the lake then the lake cannot be seen. This also applies. The photo shows dense fog, which could be interpreted as the camera being blocked by the fog.

**Step 3: Propositions revealing the contradiction**

Testimony 4 states Lotta used binoculars to look at the lake after hearing the noise. This implies she *should* have been able to see something, even with the fog. The contradiction is that she claims to have looked with binoculars, but the photo taken at the same time shows a view so obscured it's difficult to see anything. Prop 15 is relevant because the fog could be considered a blocking factor, preventing clear visibility.

**Step 4: Spatial reasoning**

The core spatial reasoning is about visibility and observation. Lotta's testimony implies a degree of visibility that is inconsistent with the visibility depicted in the Lake Photo. The use of binoculars further strengthens the expectation of visibility, making the contradiction more significant.

The spatial reasoning involves understanding the implications of a chase. A chase implies proximity and a relatively consistent direction of movement. Prop 13: If the bloodstain is in the meeting room then the meeting room covers the bloodstain. This supports Franziska's claim, but doesn't negate the storeroom's potential relevance.

Prop 1 and Prop 2 are key here. Prop 1 establishes the storeroom is a separate location from the meeting room. Prop 2 establishes a connection between them. Franziska's testimony (Testimony 3) limits the crime scene to the meeting room, ignoring the storeroom's potential involvement. Evidence 17 (Ms. Hart's Picture) suggests the storeroom was a location of interest, potentially involving the victim and/or the culprit. The contradiction arises because Franziska's statement excludes the storeroom, while the evidence places a crucial element of the crime within it.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning is based on the physical separation of locations. Franziska's statement creates a spatial boundary, claiming the crime was confined to the meeting room. The photograph introduces a new spatial element – the storeroom – and places a significant piece of evidence (the red raincoat) within it. LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 5 ("I put my back to the wall to fight, but the thief's blow landed upon my third eye.") and the general implications of Evidence 6 (Shichishito - DeMasque knocked Atmey out with a blow to the back of the head). Testimony 5 implies Atmey was facing the thief when struck, while Evidence 6 suggests a blow to the back of the head, indicating Atmey's back was to the thief. This creates a spatial contradiction: Atmey claims to have been facing the thief, but the method of attack suggests he was not.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 6: "If the thief has their back to Atmey then Atmey cannot see the thief." This proposition is relevant, but doesn't directly address the contradiction. It focuses on visibility, not the direction of the blow.
Prop 5: "If the thief is facing Atmey then Atmey can see the thief." This is also relevant, but again, focuses on visibility.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Neither Prop 5 nor Prop 6 directly reveal the contradiction. They describe conditions for visibility, not the direction of an attack. The contradiction is about the *spatial relationship* between Atmey and the thief *during the attack*, not whether Atmey could see the thief.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through basic spatial reasoning. Testimony 5 states Atmey was facing the thief. Evidence 6 describes a blow to the back of the head. A blow to the back of the head implies the attacker was *behind* Atmey, not facing him. These two statements are spatially incompatible.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The immediate retreat into the hatch after shooting Simeon seems inconsistent with the prior interaction and instruction given to Gavelle.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 5: The double is in front of Simeon - This is consistent with the shooting.
Prop 6: The double is inside the hatch to the 51st floor - This is consistent with Testimony 4.
Prop 7: The 51st floor is behind the hatch - This is a general statement and doesn't directly address the contradiction.
Prop 8: Simeon is behind the Bigg Building - This is irrelevant to the contradiction.
Prop 9: The monster footprints are in front of the broken concrete - Irrelevant.
Prop 10: Shaun Fenn is in the same room as the monster footprints - Irrelevant.

Prop 12: The Taurusaurus Head is above the roof of the production trailer - Irrelevant.
Prop 13: The Taurusaurus Plushie is in front of President Wang - Irrelevant.
Prop 14: The Security Camera Footage shows President Wang in front of the elevator - Irrelevant.
Prop 15: The Security Camera Footage shows Judge Gavelle in front of the elevator - Irrelevant.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist**

Prop 4, "Simeon is behind the hatch to the 51st floor," directly contradicts Testimony 4, which states the double entered the hatch *after* shooting Simeon. If Simeon were behind the hatch, the double could not have immediately entered it after shooting him. The testimonies imply a close proximity and immediate retreat, which is spatially impossible if Simeon were already behind the hatch.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):

**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Crime Photo 2 (Evidence 2) and Phoenix's Testimony (Evidence 6). Crime Photo 2 states the victim's watch stopped at 3:05, indicating the time of death. Phoenix's Testimony (Evidence 6) states the victim fell on top of his umbrella and there was a loud sound. However, Crime Photo 2 shows the umbrella lying "in a distance," suggesting it wasn't directly involved in the fall or immediately adjacent to the victim's body. The spatial relationship described in the testimony (umbrella directly involved in the fall) contradicts the spatial relationship depicted in the crime photo (umbrella at a distance).

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 15: If Doug Swallow is inside the broken power cable and the broken power cable is disconnected from the Ivy U. building and Winston Payne is inside the Ivy U. building then Doug Swallow is disconnected from the Winston Payne. Prop 3: If Doug Swallow is hit from behind then Doug Swallow falls forward. This also doesn't apply for the same reason.
Prop 5: If Doug Swallow falls onto the umbrella and the umbrella is covered by the Ivy U. building then Doug Swallow is covered by the Ivy U. building. This proposition is relevant. Phoenix's testimony implies the fall *onto* the umbrella, but the crime photo shows the umbrella at a distance, not "covered" by the Ivy U. building.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 5 applies. Phoenix's testimony suggests the umbrella was directly involved in the fall, implying Doug Swallow fell *onto* it. If Doug Swallow fell onto the umbrella, and the umbrella was covered by the Ivy U. building (as implied by Prop 5), then Doug Swallow would also be covered by the Ivy U. building. However, Crime Photo 2 shows the umbrella at a distance, not covered by the Ivy U. building, and Doug Swallow is lying near the broken power cable, not under the umbrella or covered by the building. This contradiction highlights the inconsistency in the spatial description of the scene.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through direct spatial comparison. The testimony describes a close spatial relationship between the victim and the umbrella during the fall. The crime photo depicts a distant spatial relationship between the victim and the umbrella. This discrepancy in spatial proximity constitutes the contradiction.

Furthermore, if she fell into Eagle River, her scarf would be wet and muddy (Prop 13). However, Evidence 4 shows a dry and clean coat and no scarf, directly contradicting these implications.

The spatial reasoning involves understanding the location of the sharp rock bed under the bridge and the proximity of Eagle River. If someone is pushed from the bridge, they are likely to land on the rocks or fall into the river. Prop 5: If Smith is hit from the front then Smith falls backward.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

However, Crime Photo 1 shows Smith leaning *back* in the chair, which is consistent with falling forward, not backward. This contradicts Olga's testimony. Prop 4 suggests that if Smith was hit from behind, he would fall forward, which is consistent with Crime Photo 1.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through basic spatial reasoning. Olga's testimony describes an attacker (Wright) in front of the victim (Smith), while the crime scene photo depicts a victim leaning back, a posture more consistent with being struck from behind or the side. The two spatial descriptions are incompatible.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 3 ("That's when Mr. Godot arrived at the Inner Temple for the first time!") and Testimony 0 ("Pearly was also stuck on the Inner Temple side that night!"). Testimony 0 implies Pearl was *already* on the Inner Temple side. Testimony 3 states Godot arrived *at* the Inner Temple, implying he wasn't there previously. If Pearl was already there, and Godot arrived, it suggests a temporal and potentially spatial contradiction - Godot arriving *after* Pearl was already present in the Inner Temple.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 1: Godot can potentially see Pearl Fey if Pearl Fey is in the same room as Godot. This is relevant, as the Inner Temple could be considered a "room" in this context.
Prop 4: If Godot is in a different room from Maya then Godot cannot see Maya. This is not directly relevant as it involves Maya.
Prop 6: If Maya blocks the view of Godot then Godot cannot be seen. This is not directly relevant as it involves Maya.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 1 is relevant. If Pearl was already in the Inner Temple, and Godot arrived, then Prop 1 suggests Godot *could* have seen Pearl upon arrival. However, Testimony 4 ("He found Pearly first, and cheered her right up!") implies Godot was *looking for* Pearl, suggesting she wasn't immediately visible or obvious. This creates a spatial tension: if they were in the same area (Inner Temple), why did Godot need to "find" her? The testimonies create a spatial narrative that doesn't quite align.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction isn't about direct visibility (though Prop 1 touches on it). It's about the implied spatial relationship and timing. Testimony 0 establishes Pearl's prior presence in the Inner Temple. Testimony 3 establishes Godot's arrival *at* the Inner Temple. The contradiction arises from the implication that Godot's arrival was a separate event from Pearl's already being there.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 0, "testimony": 3}

{
  "evidence": 0,
  "testimony": 3

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Evidence 4 (Crime Photo) and Testimony 3 (Melissa's testimony). Evidence 4 states the victim was found curled up in the trunk of Fawles's car, wearing a coat and *no scarf*. Testimony 3 states the killer hid the body in the trunk. This seems consistent. However, Evidence 5 (Scarf) describes a wet and muddy scarf found on Dusky Bridge. Testimony 0 and 1 describe Fawles picking up the victim and carrying her to the car. If the scarf was found on the bridge, and Fawles carried the victim to the car, it implies the scarf was *not* in the trunk with the victim. This creates a spatial contradiction: the scarf is either on the bridge or in the trunk, but not both.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 4: If the victim is curled up in the trunk then the victim is inside the trunk. This supports the evidence.
Prop 6: If the scarf is wet and muddy then the scarf is under the bridge. This supports the evidence.
Prop 13: If Fawles picks up Valerie in his arms then Fawles is near Valerie. This supports the testimony.

However, none of these propositions directly reveal the contradiction. The contradiction arises from the *combination* of the scarf's location (bridge) and the victim's location (trunk), and the actions described in the testimonies.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

While Props 4, 6, and 13 are relevant, they don't directly expose the contradiction. The contradiction is not about *if* something is in a location, but about the *inconsistency* of the scarf's location given the testimony of Fawles carrying the victim.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning involves understanding the implied movement of the victim and the scarf. Testimony 3 places the body in the trunk. Evidence 5 places the scarf on the bridge. Testimony 0 and 1 describe Fawles carrying the victim. If Fawles carried the victim from the bridge to the car, the scarf should have been with her in the car, and therefore in the trunk. The fact that it wasn't creates a spatial inconsistency. The scarf's location contradicts the implied sequence of events.

**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 4 ("A shot! Cause of death: Single bullet to the right temple.").  Testimony 4 states the victim (Pal Meraktis) was hit from the *front*, while the autopsy report indicates the bullet entered the *right temple*.  These are spatially opposing directions. If the bullet entered the right temple, it wasn't a direct frontal impact.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 2: "If Pal Meraktis is hit from the front then Pal Meraktis falls backward." This proposition is relevant. Testimony 4 implies a frontal hit, which, according to Prop 2, should result in Pal Meraktis falling backward. However, we have no information about Pal Meraktis's fall direction.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 2 is relevant. Testimony 4 claims a frontal shot, which, according to Prop 2, *should* have caused Pal Meraktis to fall backward. The autopsy report doesn't mention the direction of the fall, but the bullet entering the right temple contradicts the implied direction of the fall from a frontal shot. The testimony and the autopsy report present conflicting spatial information about the impact.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through basic spatial reasoning. A "frontal" impact means hitting something directly in front. A bullet entering the right temple is not a frontal impact. These are mutually exclusive spatial descriptions of the same event.

The Crime Scene Diagram indicates the Fox Chamber (where the demon presumably was imprisoned) is to the *east* of the corridor, and L'Belle fled down the hallway to his *right*. This implies L'Belle fled *towards* the Fox Chamber, not away from it. This is a spatial contradiction: fleeing *towards* the source of terror is inconsistent with fleeing *in terror* from it.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 5: "If the Crime Scene Diagram blocks the view of the Fox Chamber then the Fox Chamber cannot be seen." This proposition doesn't directly address the contradiction, as it only concerns blocking a view, not the direction of escape.
Prop 11: "If the Crime Photo is inside the Fox Chamber and the Fox Chamber is to the right of the corridor then the Crime Photo is to the right of the corridor." This is irrelevant as it concerns the location of a photo, not L'Belle's movement.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which and explain**

None of the provided spatial propositions directly resolve or explain the contradiction. The contradiction is based on a simple spatial relationship: fleeing *towards* something terrifying is illogical.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning**

The spatial reasoning is straightforward. Testimony 4 describes a flight *away* from a perceived threat. Evidence 5, combined with the general layout of the area, suggests that the direction of L'Belle's flight was *towards* the Fox Chamber, the source of the threat. This creates a spatial inconsistency.

{"evidence": 5, "testimony": 4}

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Evidence 5 ("Crime Scene Notes") and Testimony 4 ("There's dirt inside the costume. It must have gotten there when the body was hidden inside."). Testimony 4 implies that dirt *should* be on the front of the body if it was hidden inside the monster costume. The two statements are directly contradictory regarding the presence of dirt on the president's body.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 4: The president's body is inside the monster costume.
Prop 5: The president's body is behind Shaun.
Prop 1: The monster costume is in front of the dirt.

Prop 4 directly relates to the location of the body. Prop 1 describes the costume's position relative to the dirt. Prop 5 describes the body's position relative to Shaun.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which and explain**

Prop 1 ("The monster costume is in front of the dirt") and Evidence 5 ("...and yet there's no dirt on the front of the body") reveal the contradiction. If the costume is *in front of* the dirt, and the body was inside the costume, then the front of the body *should* have dirt on it. The absence of dirt on the front of the body, as stated in the evidence, contradicts the spatial relationship described in Prop 1. Testimony 4 further reinforces this contradiction by stating the dirt *should* be on the body if it was hidden inside the costume.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain**

Not applicable, as Prop 1 directly addresses the spatial relationship at the heart of the contradiction.

**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 1 ("That woman, she came back down on the elevator alone.") and Testimony 2 ("After that, there was no sign of the president getting on the elevator at all!"). These statements directly contradict each other regarding the president's use of the elevator. Testimony 1 implies the president was with the woman when she used the elevator, while Testimony 2 states the president never used the elevator.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 4: If President Wang was inside the elevator and the elevator is in front of the roof then President Wang is in front of the roof.
Prop 5: If President Wang was inside the elevator and the elevator is behind the roof then President Wang is behind the roof.
Prop 12: If the Security Camera Footage shows President Wang and Judge Gavèlle in front of the elevator then the elevator is behind President Wang and Judge Gavèlle.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 12 is relevant. The Security Camera Footage (Evidence 10) shows President Wang and Judge Gavèlle in front of the elevator. This, according to Prop 12, means the elevator is *behind* them. However, Testimony 1 states the woman (Judge Gavèlle) came down on the elevator alone, implying the president was *with* her on the elevator. This contradicts the implication of Prop 12, which places the elevator behind them. If the president was with her on the elevator, he would have also been descending with her, and thus would have been seen using the elevator. Testimony 2 then states he never used the elevator, creating a spatial and logical contradiction.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning is straightforward. The elevator's position relative to the individuals is key. Testimony 1 suggests a shared descent via the elevator, placing the president and Judge Gavèlle together within the elevator's space. Testimony 2 denies this shared descent, placing the president outside the elevator's space and thus not moving downward with the elevator.

**Step 5: Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 10, "testimony": 2}

This directly contradicts the implication of Athena's sole involvement.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 7: If Athena Cykes's fingerprints are on the lighter then Athena Cykes held the lighter.
Prop 9: If Clay Terran's fingerprints are on the lighter then Clay Terran held the lighter.

Both propositions are relevant. Prop 7 supports Fulbright's argument that Athena held the lighter, but Prop 9 introduces a conflicting element: Clay Terran also held the lighter.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 7 and Prop 9 both highlight the contradiction. Prop 7 supports Fulbright's claim, but Prop 9 undermines it by establishing that Clay Terran also handled the lighter. The presence of Terran's fingerprints and blood on the lighter, alongside Athena's, casts doubt on Fulbright's singular focus on Athena as the culprit. It suggests a more complex scenario where multiple individuals interacted with the weapon.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction isn't based on spatial relationships like distance or direction, but on the conflicting information regarding who handled the evidence. The spatial reasoning is simply that the testimony presents a singular attribution of the lighter to Athena, while the evidence reveals a shared interaction with Clay Terran.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 1 ("That's when I saw Juniper dragging a large mat from behind the stage.") and Testimony 3 ("She was heading towards me, so I assume she was on her way to the maintenance area."). O'Conner states Juniper was dragging a mat *from behind the stage* and then assumes she was heading *towards him* (and thus, towards the vantage point near the maintenance area). This implies a spatial relationship: Juniper starts behind the stage, drags the mat, and then moves towards O'Conner. However, if she was dragging a large mat, it would be difficult to move quickly and directly towards O'Conner. The act of dragging a mat would significantly impede her progress.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 2: "If Juniper was heading towards O'Conner then Juniper was on her way to the vantage point." This proposition is relevant. O'Conner claims Juniper was heading towards him, and therefore, according to Prop 2, she was on her way to the vantage point.

Prop 5: "If Juniper dragged a large mat from behind the stage then the mat was behind Juniper." This is also relevant.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 5 states that if Juniper dragged a mat from behind the stage, the mat would be *behind* her. Testimony 1 explicitly states she was dragging the mat. Therefore, the mat should have been behind her as she moved. However, Testimony 3 implies she was moving *towards* O'Conner, which suggests she was moving *forward*, not dragging something behind her. The act of dragging a large mat would make it difficult to move quickly and directly towards O'Conner. The two testimonies create a spatial conflict: dragging a mat implies a slower, more cumbersome movement, while heading towards someone implies a more direct and rapid movement.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction is identified through spatial reasoning. Dragging a large mat inherently creates a spatial constraint – it's difficult to move quickly while dragging something. Testimony 3 describes a movement towards O'Conner, implying a certain speed and direction. These two descriptions are spatially incompatible.

## OBJECTIVE:
**FIND THE CONTRADICTION:** Which evidence and testimony contradict each other?

## CONSTRAINT:
**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 3 ("The bloke wot owned the place was 'oldin' a gun, so 'e should 'ave just fired instead of yellin' at the girl!") and Evidence 9 (Autopsy Report - confirms instant death due to a single bullet wound from the back). Nash's testimony implies Windibank was holding a gun and *yelling* instead of firing. However, the autopsy report states Windibank was shot from the back, indicating he was not in a position to be yelling and holding a gun simultaneously. The spatial implication is that if Windibank was yelling, he was facing away from the shooter, which is inconsistent with being shot from the back.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 7: "If Windibank is hit from behind then Windibank falls forward" applies. This proposition establishes a spatial relationship between the direction of the wound and the direction of the fall. If Windibank was shot from behind (as the autopsy report states), he would have fallen forward. Nash's testimony suggests Windibank was yelling, implying he was facing forward, which contradicts the expected forward fall after being shot from behind.

**Step 3: Propositions revealing the contradiction**

Prop 7 directly reveals the contradiction. The autopsy report confirms Windibank was shot from behind, which, according to Prop 7, should have resulted in him falling forward. Nash's testimony describes Windibank yelling, implying he was facing forward. These two pieces of information are spatially incompatible.

**Step 4: Spatial reasoning**

The contradiction arises from the spatial relationship between the direction of the gunshot wound and Windibank's actions. A gunshot to the back necessitates a backward trajectory, which would likely cause a forward fall. Yelling, however, implies facing forward. These two spatial positions are mutually exclusive in the context of the event.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 9, "testimony": 3}

PARSED RESULT:
--------------------
{
  "evidence": 9,
  "testimony": 3
}

The spatial reasoning suggests a disconnect between the calm act of placing a book and the forceful act of swinging a suitcase.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction arises from the spatial relationship between the act of violence (swinging a suitcase) and the seemingly deliberate action of placing a coffee-stained book back on the shelf. The two actions are spatially incompatible given the force described in the testimonies. It's improbable that someone would carefully place a book after a violent struggle.

**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 2 ("While I was searching, I heard a scream coming from the next room over.") and Testimony 3 ("Although I was in the next room, I was unable to catch a glimpse of the Yatagarasu."). Shih-na claims to have been in the adjacent room while a scream occurred, yet she didn't see the Yatagarasu. This implies a spatial inconsistency: if she was *in* the next room, she should have had a reasonable opportunity to see someone, especially if they were causing a disturbance (screaming). The inability to see anyone despite being in the adjacent room suggests she wasn't actually in the next room, or that her position within the room was obstructed.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 29: If the revolving fireplace wall on Babahl's side has been used then Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall and into the next room over.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 29 directly relates to the contradiction. Testimony 2 states Shih-na heard a scream from the "next room over." Prop 29 states that the revolving fireplace wall was used, and that it allowed Yatagarasu to escape *into* the next room over. If Prop 29 is true, and Shih-na was truly in the adjacent room, she *should* have seen Yatagarasu escaping through the fireplace wall. Her inability to see anyone, as stated in Testimony 3, contradicts the implication of Prop 29. This suggests either Prop 29 is false (the fireplace wall wasn't used), or Shih-na's location was misrepresented in Testimony 2.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

N/A - Prop 29 directly addresses the spatial contradiction.

**Step 5: Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 29, "testimony": 3}

  "testimony": 3

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):

**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 1 ("In the lounge, we saw a figure standing in the middle of the room, and Terran on the floor.") and the implication of Evidence 1 (Terran's Autopsy Report: "The victim was discovered in the third-floor boarding lounge in the main building."). Testimony 1 places both Terran (on the floor) and a figure in the *lounge*, implying the lounge is the location of the incident. However, Evidence 1 states Terran was *discovered* in the boarding lounge. This suggests a potential discrepancy in the timing or location of events described by Cosmos. If Cosmos saw a figure and Terran on the floor, it implies the incident occurred *while* Cosmos was present in the lounge. The autopsy report, however, describes a discovery *after* the incident.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions apply**

Prop 4: "If the figure fired at Cosmos and Arme then the figure is in the same room as Cosmos and Arme" - This is relevant because Testimony 2 states the figure fired at Cosmos and Arme.

Prop 12: "If the figure is standing in the middle of the room and Terran is on the floor then the figure can potentially see Terran" - This is also relevant, as Testimony 1 describes this scenario.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Prop 12 is key here. Testimony 1 states Cosmos saw a figure standing in the middle of the room and Terran on the floor. Prop 12 states that if this is the case, the figure *can potentially see Terran*. However, the autopsy report (Evidence 1) indicates Terran was *discovered* in the lounge, implying he was already on the floor when Cosmos arrived. This creates a temporal contradiction. If Cosmos saw the figure *and* Terran on the floor, Cosmos would have witnessed the event. The autopsy report suggests Cosmos found Terran already in that state, implying Cosmos did not witness the fall.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning centers on the implied location and timing of events. Testimony 1 establishes a spatial relationship (figure and Terran in the lounge) and implies a temporal sequence (Cosmos witnessing the scene). Evidence 1, however, describes a discovery, suggesting a different temporal sequence where Cosmos arrived *after* Terran was already on the floor. The contradiction arises from the conflicting spatial and temporal implications of these two pieces of information.
